The example

Relations: x*x*x

Algebra of dimension 3 over GF(3)

Resolution

Resolution used: chouhy-solotar
the fast GF(p) bar basis exceeded max_cells at this depth; auto reroutes to the Chouhy-Solotar resolution over the admissible presentation (exact, certified per instance) — max_cells is the engine's safety cap on the size of one differential matrix, counted in cells = rows × columns; a computation that would exceed it is refused or rerouted instead of exhausting memory (raise max_cells= if you have the RAM).
defining relations: 1

Worked resolution steps

These steps resolve the algebra itself: P• → A is a projective resolution of A as a bimodule — a module over the enveloping algebra Ae = A ⊗ Aop — built with the Chouhy–Solotar resolution of the admissible presentation. Collapsing it computes both sides: HHn = Hn HomAe(P•, A) and HHn = Hn(P• ⊗Ae A). Cn is the (co)chain space this resolution induces in degree n; the matrices below are its differentials (rows: target basis, columns: source basis).

Hochschild cohomology

What the engine computes: Hochschild cohomology as the cohomology of Hom_{A^e}(P_•, A), where P_• → A is the Chouhy–Solotar projective bimodule resolution with P_n = ⊕_σ A e_{o(σ)} ⊗ e_{t(σ)} A (σ over the degree-n ambiguity chains). Degree n collapses to the corner space C^n = ⊕_σ e_{o(σ)} A e_{t(σ)}. A basis element v ⊗ p pairs a path v ∈ A with a chain word p = a_1·a_2·…. The word p NAMES the free generator of P_n attached to that degree-n chain (an iterated overlap of the relations): it is not the product of its letters in A -- that product is typically zero there, the generator never is. A cochain [p → v] sends THAT generator to v ∈ A. Here ⊗ is the tensor product over k, and · inside a word is composition of arrows along a path (left to right) -- not a scalar.

n0123456789101112
dim HH^n3333333333333

engine: Chouhy-Solotar — the Chouhy–Solotar projective bimodule resolution built from the admissible presentation, certified per instance (d∘d = 0 + the order gate).

Interpretation of the spaces (the classical dictionary):

  • HH⁰(A) = Z(A), the CENTRE of A: the classes are the central elements z (those with za = az for all a in A).
  • HH¹(A) = Der(A) / Inn(A), the OUTER DERIVATIONS: each class is a derivation D : A → A (a k-linear map with the Leibniz rule D(ab) = D(a) b + a D(b)), determined by its values on the arrow generators, taken modulo the inner derivations a ↦ ax − xa.
  • HH²(A) classifies the INFINITESIMAL DEFORMATIONS of A: each class is a 2-cocycle μ(a, b) giving a first-order (square-zero) deformation of the multiplication a * b = ab + t·μ(a, b); the coboundaries are the trivial (gauge) deformations.
  • HHⁿ(A) controls the higher obstructions to deforming A (its Yoneda product with HH² carries the obstruction cocycles); homological framing.

HH⁰ = Z(A): each class is a central element z (za = az for all a in A).

  • α10\alpha^{0}_{1}: e_1
  • α20\alpha^{0}_{2}: x
  • α30\alpha^{0}_{3}: x*x

HH¹ = Der(A) / Inn(A): each class is an outer derivation, read off as D(arrow) = value.

  • α11\alpha^{1}_{1}: D(x) = e_1
  • α21\alpha^{1}_{2}: D(x) = x
  • α31\alpha^{1}_{3}: D(x) = x*x

inner derivations (the coboundaries a ↦ ax − xa): dimension 0 = rank δ⁰.

HH² = infinitesimal deformations: each class is the 2-cocycle μ(a, b) of a first-order deformation a * b = ab + t·μ(a, b).

  • α12\alpha^{2}_{1}: μ(x·x·x) = e_1
  • α22\alpha^{2}_{2}: μ(x·x·x) = x
  • α32\alpha^{2}_{3}: μ(x·x·x) = x*x

Explicit representatives by degree — each class written over the ordered (co)chain basis, with the differential that annihilates it. Coordinate vectors are in the JSON.

Hochschild homology

What the engine computes: Hochschild homology as the homology of A ⊗_{A^e} P_•, where P_• → A is the Chouhy–Solotar projective bimodule resolution with P_n = ⊕_σ A e_{o(σ)} ⊗ e_{t(σ)} A. Degree n collapses to the corner space C_n = ⊕_σ e_{t(σ)} A e_{o(σ)}. A basis element v ⊗ p pairs a path v ∈ A with a chain word p = a_1·a_2·…. The word p NAMES the free generator of P_n attached to that degree-n chain (an iterated overlap of the relations): it is not the product of its letters in A -- that product is typically zero there, the generator never is. Here ⊗ is the tensor product over k, and · inside a word is composition of arrows along a path (left to right) -- not a scalar.

n0123456789101112
dim HH_n3333333333333

engine: Chouhy-Solotar — the Chouhy–Solotar projective bimodule resolution built from the admissible presentation, certified per instance (d∘d = 0 + the order gate).

Interpretation of the spaces (the classical dictionary):

  • HH₀(A) = A / [A, A], the COMMUTATOR QUOTIENT: the classes are the residues of A modulo the subspace spanned by the commutators ab − ba.
  • HHₙ(A) is the n-th Hochschild homology, Tor^{A^e}_n(A, A) -- a derived-functor / cyclic-theory invariant (homological framing).

HH₀ = A / [A, A]: each class is the residue of an element modulo the commutators ab − ba.

  • z10z^{0}_{1}: e_1
  • z20z^{0}_{2}: x
  • z30z^{0}_{3}: x*x

Explicit representatives by degree — each class written over the ordered (co)chain basis, with the differential that annihilates it. Coordinate vectors are in the JSON.

Cyclic homology

n012345678
dim HC_n314253647

engine: hanlab engine (F_p fast rank) — quiverlab's exact GF(p) linear-algebra engine: it assembles the boundary/coboundary matrices of the chosen (co)chain complex with integer entries mod p and computes their exact rank by Gaussian elimination mod p; every dimension follows by rank-nullity -- nothing numerical, no floating point.

Interpretation of the spaces (the classical dictionary):

  • HC₀(A) = A / [A, A]: the same space as HH₀, read as the TRACE FUNCTIONALS on A (a trace τ with τ(ab) = τ(ba) is exactly a linear form on A / [A, A]).
  • HCₙ(A) is cyclic homology in degree n, the homology of Connes' (b, B) total complex -- it packages the S, B, I periodicity of the Hochschild theory (homological framing).

Explicit representatives by degree — each class as its representative, with the total differential b+B that annihilates it. The total complex Tot_n and its ordered basis (into which the coordinate vectors index) are in the JSON.

The total complex Tot_n = C_n ⊕ C_{n-2} ⊕ … and the ordered basis of each Tot_n (into which the coordinate vectors index) are recorded in the JSON.

HC0HC_{0} in degree 0

Basis classes, each as its labelled representative:

  • z10z^{0}_{1} = col C_0: e_1
  • z20z^{0}_{2} = col C_0: x
  • z30z^{0}_{3} = col C_0: x*x
D0:Tot0→Tot0D_{0} : \mathrm{Tot}_{0} \to \mathrm{Tot}_{0}

D_0 = 0 on Tot_0 = C_0; every 0-chain is a cycle (HC_0 = A/[A,A]).

Verification: each z^{1}_i is a cycle of the total complex: applying D_{1} = b + B to its coordinate vector gives 0.

Connes, Alain (1985). Non-commutative differential geometry. Publications Math'ematiques de l'IH'ES 62, 41-144.

Connes differentials

each induced Connes differential B_n: HH_n → HH_{n+1} is written on the recorded homology bases -- rows index HH_{n+1}, columns index HH_n; the entries are basis-dependent. The homology cycle classes z^n_j (z^n_j = the j-th basis class of HH_n) are listed explicitly by degree above as combinations of the ordered basis elements.

engine: engine (b,B) GF(3) — the exact (b, B) mixed-complex engine on the normalized bar complex: b is the Hochschild boundary, B the Connes boundary.

Connes, Alain (1985). Non-commutative differential geometry. Publications Math'ematiques de l'IH'ES 62, 41-144.

Cup product tables

α^n_j denotes the j-th basis class of HH^n (superscript = degree, subscript = index). Every table line states α^p_i ∪ α^q_j = Σ_k c·α^{p+q}_k, relative to the recorded basis bar/GF(3); the constants c are basis-dependent. Each class is listed explicitly by degree above as a combination of the ordered basis elements; the coordinate vectors are recorded in the JSON.

Coefficients are shown as balanced representatives mod 3 (a residue c > 3/2 is written c-3); the JSON record keeps the raw residues.

Note: this table's classes are enumerated over its recorded basis (bar/GF(3)); the Hochschild sections above were computed on a different route (Chouhy-Solotar, Chouhy-Solotar). The two class lists are independent enumerations — their indices do not correspond — and no coordinates from different resolutions are mixed inside this table; the dimension tables agree because dimensions are basis-independent.

The Hochschild (co)homology basis classes, over the chain bases enumerated in the sections above:

HH*∪HH*→HH*HH^{*} \cup HH^{*} \to HH^{*}

One row per left class and one column per right class, ordered degree-major (the degree is the class' superscript); a heavier rule marks each degree boundary.

The cup product is graded-commutative.

— marks a cell whose target degree lies beyond the computed window (not computed); a computed vanishing product is shown as 0.

engine: hanlab engine (F_p fast rank) — quiverlab's exact GF(p) linear-algebra engine: it assembles the boundary/coboundary matrices of the chosen (co)chain complex with integer entries mod p and computes their exact rank by Gaussian elimination mod p; every dimension follows by rank-nullity -- nothing numerical, no floating point.

Gerstenhaber, Murray (1963). The cohomology structure of an associative ring. Annals of Mathematics. Second Series 78, 267-288.

Cap product tables

α^p_j denotes the j-th basis class of HH^p (cohomology) and z^n_j the j-th of HH_n (homology). Every table line states α^p_i ∩ z^n_j = Σ_k c·z^{n-p}_k, relative to the recorded basis bar/GF(3); the constants c are basis-dependent. Each class is listed explicitly by degree above as a combination of the ordered basis elements; the coordinate vectors are recorded in the JSON.

Coefficients are shown as balanced representatives mod 3 (a residue c > 3/2 is written c-3); the JSON record keeps the raw residues.

Note: this table's classes are enumerated over its recorded basis (bar/GF(3)); the Hochschild sections above were computed on a different route (Chouhy-Solotar, Chouhy-Solotar). The two class lists are independent enumerations — their indices do not correspond — and no coordinates from different resolutions are mixed inside this table; the dimension tables agree because dimensions are basis-independent.

engine: hanlab engine (F_p fast rank) — quiverlab's exact GF(p) linear-algebra engine: it assembles the boundary/coboundary matrices of the chosen (co)chain complex with integer entries mod p and computes their exact rank by Gaussian elimination mod p; every dimension follows by rank-nullity -- nothing numerical, no floating point.

Gerstenhaber, Murray (1963). The cohomology structure of an associative ring. Annals of Mathematics. Second Series 78, 267-288.

Gerstenhaber bracket tables

α^n_j denotes the j-th basis class of HH^n (superscript = degree, subscript = index). Every table line states [α^p_i, α^q_j] = Σ_k c·α^{p+q-1}_k in degree p+q−1, relative to the recorded basis bar/GF(3); the constants c are basis-dependent. Each class is listed explicitly by degree above as a combination of the ordered basis elements; the coordinate vectors are recorded in the JSON.

Coefficients are shown as balanced representatives mod 3 (a residue c > 3/2 is written c-3); the JSON record keeps the raw residues.

Note: this table's classes are enumerated over its recorded basis (bar/GF(3)); the Hochschild sections above were computed on a different route (Chouhy-Solotar, Chouhy-Solotar). The two class lists are independent enumerations — their indices do not correspond — and no coordinates from different resolutions are mixed inside this table; the dimension tables agree because dimensions are basis-independent.

The Hochschild (co)homology basis classes, over the chain bases enumerated in the sections above:

[HH*,HH*]→HH*[HH^{*}, HH^{*}] \to HH^{*}

One row per left class and one column per right class, ordered degree-major (the degree is the class' superscript); a heavier rule marks each degree boundary.

All squares are 0; the Gerstenhaber bracket is graded-antisymmetric.

— marks a cell whose target degree lies beyond the computed window (not computed); a computed vanishing product is shown as 0.

bracket structure constants are served for arguments of total degree ≤ 8 — the largest window this bar-route computation certifies; a cell beyond it is marked —. The bracket is computed entirely on the bar (co)chain route.

engine: hanlab engine (F_p fast rank) — quiverlab's exact GF(p) linear-algebra engine: it assembles the boundary/coboundary matrices of the chosen (co)chain complex with integer entries mod p and computes their exact rank by Gaussian elimination mod p; every dimension follows by rank-nullity -- nothing numerical, no floating point.

Gerstenhaber, Murray (1963). The cohomology structure of an associative ring. Annals of Mathematics. Second Series 78, 267-288.

The projectives and injectives of A

Loewy layers stacked top to bottom; the simples Sv are omitted.

P1=S1|S1|S1dim=3,dim―=(1:3)P_{1} = \begin{matrix} S_{1} \end{matrix} \;\big|\; \begin{matrix} S_{1} \end{matrix} \;\big|\; \begin{matrix} S_{1} \end{matrix} \qquad \dim = 3,\ \underline{\dim} = (1{:}3)
I1=S1|S1|S1dim=3,dim―=(1:3)I_{1} = \begin{matrix} S_{1} \end{matrix} \;\big|\; \begin{matrix} S_{1} \end{matrix} \;\big|\; \begin{matrix} S_{1} \end{matrix} \qquad \dim = 3,\ \underline{\dim} = (1{:}3)

Loewy series and the projectives/injectives Pv, Iv follow [ASS2006] (see References).

The JSON record

This page pairs with a machine-readable record, trace.json (the “Report data (JSON)” download). Its structure:

  • quiverlab_trace_schema — integer version of this format;
  • title — the computation this record traces;
  • references — the same bibliography as the References section, as [key, formatted] pairs;
  • events — the complete ordered stream of worked steps. Each event is an object with a type field (e.g. dispatch, resolution_term, rank_step, module_term, module_differential, ext_degree, step_note, result_dims) plus that step's exact data — every matrix entry is exact (integers / rationals as strings), never floating point.

The computation's summary results (one block per requested invariant, with dimensions, matrices, and citation keys) live in the separate result.json deliverable. HTML and JSON are the only report formats — this page is the complete human-readable record.

Reading the JSON record

How to recover each object this computation produced from the machine-readable result.json. Every path is relative to the result object and uses only dot and ["key"] steps.
